w\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
and
\omega(t):=\mathcal{F}S(-t)w(t)
.
Then, we have

\int_{-T}^{T}\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}\|\mathcal{N}_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}[\omega(t)]\|_{\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}-}}\,dt\ <\ \infty.

Proof.

Similarly to Proposition
3.1
, we may estimate the whole cubic interaction

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{F}S(-t)\Big[P_{N}\big[w_{N_{1}}(t)w_{N_{2}}(t)\partial_{x}\bar{w}_{N_{3}}(t)\big]\Big]\Big\|_{\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}-}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}-}\big\|P_{N}\big[w_{N_{1}}(t)w_{N_{2}}(t)\partial_{x}\bar{w}_{N_{3}}(t)\big]\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim N^{0-}\|w_{N_{1}}(t)w_{N_{2}}(t)\partial_{x}\bar{w}_{N_{3}}(t)\|_{L^{1}}

instead of
\mathcal{N}_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}[\omega(t)]
.
Denote the maximum, median and minimum of
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}
by
N_{\max},N_{\mathrm{med}},N_{\min}
, respectively.
When the set
\{(\xi,\xi_{l}):\xi=\xi_{123},\,\xi\in I_{N},\,\xi_{l}\in I_{N_{l}}\}
is non-empty, it must hold either
N_{\max}\sim N_{\mathrm{med}}
or
N_{\max}\sim N\gg N_{\mathrm{med}}
.
If
N_{\max}\sim N_{\mathrm{med}}
, we apply Hölder to the
L^{1}
bound and obtain

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{0-}N_{3}\|w_{N_{\max}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{N_{\mathrm{med}}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{N_{\min}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{0-}N_{\min}^{0-}\|w_{N_{\max}}(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|w_{N_{\mathrm{med}}}(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|w(t)\|_{B^{0+}_{\infty,1}},

while if
N_{\max}\sim N\gg N_{\mathrm{med}}
we estimate the
L^{2}
bound with Hölder and Bernstein as

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}-}N_{3}\|w_{N_{\max}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{N_{\mathrm{med}}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{N_{\min}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{\max}^{0-}\|w(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}.

In each case, the resulting bound is summable in
\{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\}
.
Given the above estimates for fixed
t
, we obtain the claim by applying the Hölder inequality.
∎

By Lemma
4.11
, we can exchange the order of summation in
N,N_{l}
and integration in
t
, to obtain the equation

\omega(t)=\omega(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}[\omega]+\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}[\omega],

which holds pointwise in
t\in[-T,T]
as
\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}-}_{\xi}
-valued functions.
Then, we fix
N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}
and consider each integral:

\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}[\omega(t^{\prime})](\xi)\,dt^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{t}\psi_{N}(\xi)\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|\geq 1\end{smallmatrix}}e^{it^{\prime}\phi(\vec{\xi})}\xi_{3}\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\,dt^{\prime}.

After subtracting the resonant part, we have the trilinear term

\int_{0}^{t}\psi_{N}(\xi)\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}e^{it^{\prime}\phi(\vec{\xi})}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\,dt^{\prime},

where “NR” means the condition
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|\geq 1
, and the precise meaning of the restriction “
|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M
” depends on the relation between
N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}
.
Now, we need to justify the following NFR operation:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}e^{it^{\prime}\phi}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\,dt^{\prime}

(4.11)

\displaystyle=\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\bigg|_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t^{\prime}=t}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\Big\{\zeta_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})

\displaystyle+\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\zeta_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})+\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\zeta_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\Big\}\,dt^{\prime},

where
\omega_{N_{l}}=\psi_{N_{l}}\omega\in W^{1,4}(-T,T;L^{2}(\hat{\mathcal{M}}))\cap C([-T,T];L^{2}(\hat{\mathcal{M}}))
and

\zeta_{N_{l}}:=\partial_{t}\omega_{N_{l}}=\psi_{N_{l}}\big(\mathcal{N}[\omega]+\mathcal{R}[\omega]\big)\in L^{4}_{T}L^{2}(\hat{\mathcal{M}})

for each
N_{l}
.
Since we do not know if
\omega_{N_{l}}(t,\xi)
is continuously differentiable in
t
(neither pointwise in
\xi
nor in the
L^{2}_{\xi}
sense), the above operation (i.e., integration by parts and application of the product rule) must be interpreted in a weak sense, which is done in the next lemma.

Lemma 4.12
.

The equality (
4.11
) holds for any
t\in[-T,T]
and
\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}
.

Proof.

We write (
4.11
) as

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}e^{it^{\prime}\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}=\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\bigg|_{0}^{t}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}g(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime},

where

\displaystyle f(t,\vec{\xi})

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\omega_{N_{1}}(t,\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t,\xi_{2})\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t,\xi_{3}),

\displaystyle g(t,\vec{\xi})

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\Big\{\zeta_{N_{1}}(t,\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t,\xi_{2})\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t,\xi_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 90.0pt+\omega_{N_{1}}(t,\xi_{1})\zeta_{N_{2}}(t,\xi_{2})\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t,\xi_{3})+\omega_{N_{1}}(t,\xi_{1})\omega_{N_{2}}(t,\xi_{2})\zeta^{*}_{N_{3}}(t,\xi_{3})\Big\}.

Note that every integral in
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}
in (
4.11
) converges absolutely for each
\xi
due to the localization.
We show (
4.11
) by taking the limit
h\to 0
of the following identity:

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}\frac{f(t^{\prime}+h,\vec{\xi})-f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})}{h}\,dt^{\prime}\ =\ \int_{h}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{i(t^{\prime}-h)\phi}-e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{ih\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}\\
+\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{i(t^{\prime}-h)\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}-\frac{1}{h}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}.

Therefore, it suffices to show the following convergences as
h\to 0
(with
t+h\in[-T,T]
) for any
t\in[-T,T]
and
\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}
:

\displaystyle\int_{h}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{i(t^{\prime}-h)\phi}-e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{ih\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}\to-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}e^{it^{\prime}\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime},

(4.12)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}\!\!\!\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\!\!\!\frac{e^{i(t^{\prime}-h)\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}-\frac{1}{h}\int_{0}^{h}\!\!\!\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\!\!\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}\to\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\!\!\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\bigg|_{0}^{t},

(4.13)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}\frac{f(t^{\prime}+h,\vec{\xi})-f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})}{h}\,dt^{\prime}\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}g(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}.

(4.14)

Proof of (
4.12
): By the dominated convergence theorem, it suffices to prove that

\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}|f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})|\,dt^{\prime}<\infty.

This is easy to see from the estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}|f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})|

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{3}\Big\||\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})|*|\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})|*|\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})|\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim N_{3}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{3}\|\omega_{N_{l}}(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}

and the fact that
\omega_{N_{l}}\in C_{T}L^{2}
.

Proof of (
4.13
): We first observe that

\Big|\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{i(t^{\prime}-h)\phi}-e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}\Big|\lesssim\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}|f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})|\,dt^{\prime}\to 0\qquad(h\to 0).

It then suffices to show that the map

[-T,T]\ni~t\quad\mapsto\quad\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it\phi}}{i\phi}f(t,\vec{\xi})\quad\in L^{\infty}_{\xi}

is continuous.
On one hand, similarly to (i) we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}-e^{it\phi}}{i\phi}f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t^{\prime}-t|N_{3}\Big\||\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})|*|\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})|*|\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})|\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t^{\prime}-t|N_{3}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{3}\|\omega_{N_{l}}(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}\to 0\qquad(t^{\prime}\to t).

On the other hand, using the fact
\omega_{N_{l}}\in C_{T}L^{2}
and estimating similarly, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it\phi}}{i\phi}\Big(f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})-f(t,\vec{\xi})\Big)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}\lesssim M^{-1}N_{3}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{\|\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})-\omega_{N_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\omega_{N_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})-\omega_{N_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\omega_{N_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})-\omega_{N_{3}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\to 0\qquad(t^{\prime}\to t).

Hence, we obtain the continuity as desired.

Proof of (
4.14
): We begin with the identity

\displaystyle\frac{f(t^{\prime}+h)-f(t^{\prime})}{h}-g(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{|\phi|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &\Big(\frac{\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime}+h)-\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})}{h}-\zeta_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})\Big)\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime}+h)\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime}+h)\\
&+\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})\Big(\frac{\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime}+h)-\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})}{h}-\zeta_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})\Big)\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime}+h)\\
&+\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})\Big(\frac{\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime}+h)-\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})}{h}-\zeta^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})\Big)\\
&+\zeta_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})\Big(\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime}+h)\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime}+h)-\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})\Big)\\
&+\omega_{N_{1}}(t^{\prime})\zeta_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime})\Big(\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime}+h)-\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime})\Big)\Big\}.\end{aligned}

Then, a similar argument implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}\Big(\frac{f(t^{\prime}+h,\vec{\xi})-f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})}{h}-g(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\Big)\,dt^{\prime}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{-|t|}^{|t|}\sup_{\xi}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\frac{1}{|\phi|}\Big|\frac{f(t^{\prime}+h,\vec{\xi})-f(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})}{h}-g(t^{\prime},\vec{\xi})\Big|\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{-1}N_{3}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{\sum_{l=1}^{3}\Big\|\frac{\omega_{N_{l}}(\cdot+h)-\omega_{N_{l}}(\cdot)}{h}-\zeta_{N_{l}}(\cdot)\Big\|_{L^{4}_{|t|}L^{2}}\prod_{m\neq l}\|\omega_{N_{m}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{(k,l,m)\in\{(1,2,3),(1,3,2),(2,1,3)\}}\|\zeta_{N_{k}}\|_{L^{4}_{T}L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{l}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}}\|\omega_{N_{m}}(\cdot+h)-\omega_{N_{m}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{|t|}L^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\to 0\qquad(h\to 0),

where in the last step we have used the convergences

\|\omega_{N_{m}}(\cdot+h)-\omega_{N_{m}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T^{\prime}}L^{2}}\to 0,\qquad\Big\|\frac{\omega_{N_{l}}(\cdot+h)-\omega_{N_{l}}(\cdot)}{h}-\zeta_{N_{l}}(\cdot)\Big\|_{L^{4}_{T^{\prime}}L^{2}}\to 0\qquad(h\to 0)

for
0<T^{\prime}<T
.
11
11

11

The argument is also valid for
t=\pm T
by considering a suitable half of the interval
[-T,T]
and a one-sided limit in
h
.

The first one is by the uniform continuity of
t\mapsto\omega_{N_{m}}(t)\in L^{2}_{\xi}
, while the second one is a general property of the Sobolev space
W^{1,4}_{T}L^{2}_{\xi}
(see, e.g.,
[
2
, Corollary 1.4.39]
).
∎

After the first NFR, we have various terms such as

\displaystyle\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\psi_{N}(\xi)\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\phi}}{i\phi}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi|\gtrsim M}\xi_{3}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[\sum_{L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}}\mathcal{N}_{N_{1},L_{2},L_{2},L_{3}}[\omega(t^{\prime})](\xi_{1})\Big]\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\,dt^{\prime}.

When the dyadic frequencies
N,N_{l},L_{l}
are in a certain range, we need to apply NFR once more (see the last two subsections).
For such
N,N_{l},L_{l}
, we need to exchange the order of summation in
L_{l}
and integration in
t^{\prime},\xi_{l}
.
This is verified from the fact

\sum_{L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}}\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\xi=\xi_{123}}\big|\mathcal{N}_{N_{1},L_{2},L_{2},L_{3}}[\omega(t^{\prime})](\xi_{1})\big||\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})||\omega^{*}_{N_{3}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})|\,dt^{\prime}<\infty

for each
\xi
and
N,N_{l}
, which is a consequence of the Young inequality and Lemma
4.11
.
Then, we fix all of
N,N_{l},L_{l}
and apply NFR to the localized integral

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\psi_{N}(\xi)\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
\xi_{1}=\eta_{123}\end{smallmatrix}}

\displaystyle e^{it^{\prime}(\phi(\vec{\xi})+\phi(\vec{\eta}))}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})|\gtrsim M}\mathbf{1}_{\text{NR},\,|\phi(\vec{\xi})+\phi(\vec{\eta})|\gtrsim M}\frac{\xi_{3}\eta_{3}\psi_{N_{1}}(\xi_{1})}{\phi(\vec{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\omega_{L_{1}}(t^{\prime},\eta_{1})\omega_{L_{2}}(t^{\prime},\eta_{2})\omega_{L_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\eta_{3})\omega_{N_{2}}(t^{\prime},\xi_{2})\omega_{N_{3}}^{*}(t^{\prime},\xi_{3})\,dt^{\prime},

which is clearly absolutely convergent.
The second application of NFR is justified in exactly the same way as Lemma
4.12
, so we omit the detail.

After the second NFR, we obtain the following equation (written in a formal way):

\displaystyle\omega(t)

\displaystyle=\omega(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}[\omega]+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{R}[\omega]+\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{0}[\omega]\Big|_{0}^{t}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{R}[\omega]]

(4.15)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}[\omega]+\sum_{k=1}^{10}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{k}_{R}[\omega]+\mathcal{L}^{k}_{0}[\omega]\Big|_{0}^{t}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{k}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{R}[\omega]]+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{k}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]\Big),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}[\omega]

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]-\sum_{k=1}^{10}\mathcal{L}^{k}[\omega]

\displaystyle\;=\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{E}}_{1}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C2}}_{1,1}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C},\texttt{D}}_{1,2}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{D}}_{1,3}\Big)[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B2}}_{1,1}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B1}}_{1,2}\Big)[\omega,\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{C}}[\omega]]

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{D}}_{1,1}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C2}}_{1,3}\Big)[\omega,\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{C1}}[\omega]]+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B1},\texttt{C1}}_{1,1}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B2}}_{1,2}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B},\texttt{C1}}_{1,3}\Big)[\omega,\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{C2}}[\omega]].

Let us conclude this subsection by considering how to make sense of each term in (
4.15
).
The first two integrals of
\mathcal{R}[\omega]
and
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]
have been considered in (
4.9
).
The remaining terms are also defined as the
t
-integral of the absolutely-convergent (in
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
) sums of frequency-localized components (see Remark
4.4
for the last three terms in the first line; absolute convergence for the other terms follows similarly from the proofs of Lemmas
4.5
–
4.10
).
For instance, the last term
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{10}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]
in (
4.15
) was initially obtained in the form

\sum_{N,N_{l},K}\bigg(\sum_{L_{l}}\int_{0}^{t}\big[\text{$\mathcal{L}^{10}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]$ localized according to $N,N_{l},K,L_{l}$}\big]\bigg),

where absolute convergence of the
\xi_{l},\eta_{l}
-integrals in localized components is always clear due to localization, but the summations in
N,N_{l},K
and in
L_{l}
are still formal.
Now, the proof of the estimate (
4.6
) in Lemma
4.10
shows
12
12

12

Although we have absolute convergence in
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
in the case of
\mathcal{L}^{10}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]
, for some terms (e.g.,
\mathcal{L}^{5}_{R}[\omega]
) we use a slightly weaker
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
norm to ensure
\ell^{1}
summability in
N
.

\sum_{N,N_{l},K,L_{l}}\|\mathcal{L}^{10}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}\|S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{N}[\omega]\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}+B^{0}_{1,\infty}},

and hence, by Proposition
3.1
and the Hölder inequality,

\int_{-T}^{T}\sum_{N,N_{l},K,L_{l}}\|\mathcal{L}^{10}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}<\infty

for any
\omega(t)
such that
w(t)=S(t)\check{\omega}(t)\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
.
Hence, we can exchange the order of summation in
N,N_{l},K,L_{l}
and integration in
t
, obtaining the expression
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{10}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]
in (
4.15
).
From Proposition
3.1
and Lemmas
4.1
–
4.10
, we see that all the integrated terms in (
4.15
) (except for
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]
) are the
t
-integrals of functions in
L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

4.6
Conclusion of the first stage

We set the non-integrated part of the nonlinearity in (
4.15
) as

\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]:=\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{0}[\omega]+\sum_{j=1}^{10}\mathcal{L}^{j}_{0}[\omega],

and introduce the new unknown function

\varpi:=\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega].

Then, the equation (
4.15
) can be rewritten as

\varpi(t)=\varpi(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega],

(4.16)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}[\omega]+\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{R}[\omega]+\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{R}[\omega]]+\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}[\omega]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{10}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{j}_{R}[\omega]+\mathcal{L}^{j}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{R}[\omega]]+\mathcal{L}^{j}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]\Big)+\Big(\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]-\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]]\Big).

(
4.16
) is the equation to which we will apply infinite NFR scheme in Section
5
.

We show the regularity properties of
\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]
and
\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]
in the next two lemmas.
In particular, it turns out that
\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]
enjoys an extra smoothing property, which ensures the
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
control of the last difference term in
\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]
(note that
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]
itself admits only the
\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}+}
control; see the proof of Proposition
3.1
).

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
T\in(0,1]
.
For any
\omega
with
w=S(t)\check{\omega}\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
, the function
\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]
belongs to
C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and satisfies

\|\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\Big(M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big)\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(4.17)

where
\mathcal{P}_{0}(x):=x+x^{2}
.
Moreover, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\sup_{N}N\big\|P_{N}S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]\big\|_{L^{4}_{T}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big(1+M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big)\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}},

(4.18)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Big\|\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]-\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]]\Big\|_{L^{4}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}\Big(1+M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big)^{5}\Big(1+\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}\Big).\end{aligned}

(4.19)

Proof.

The first estimate (
4.17
) is obtained from Lemma
4.3
and the estimate (
4.5
) in Lemmas
4.8
,
4.10
.
To see that
\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}[\omega]\in C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we first notice that it is true for each localized components, which we denote by
\mathcal{N}_{0,N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}[\omega]
(see the proof of (
4.13
) in Lemma
4.12
).
Then, (except for Case
A
) we see from the proof of Lemma
4.3
that

\big\|\mathcal{N}_{0,N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}[\omega]\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\big(NN_{1}N_{2}N_{3}\big)^{0-}\|\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}.

Hence, the sum of these localized pieces is absolutely convergent in Banach space
C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and gives
\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\neg\texttt{A}}[\omega]\in C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
The same argument using the proof of (
4.5
) in Lemmas
4.8
,
4.10
shows that
\mathcal{L}_{0}^{j}[\omega]\in C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
for
1\leq j\leq 10
.

We move on to proving (
4.18
).
The Bernstein inequality bounds the left-hand side by
\sup_{N}\|\psi_{N}\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]\|_{L^{4}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}}
, and we start with this norm for most cases.
Concerning
\mathcal{L}^{j}_{0}
with
1\leq j\leq 10
, we see from the proof of the estimate (
4.6
) in Lemmas
4.8
,
4.10
that

\|\mathcal{L}^{j}_{1}[\omega,\zeta]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{4}\|z\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}},

and hence
13
13

13

Note that each of five terms in
\mathcal{L}^{j}_{1}[\omega,\zeta]
has been estimated separately.

\|\mathcal{L}^{j}_{0}[\omega]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq\|\mathcal{L}^{j}_{1}[\omega,\langle\xi\rangle\omega]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{5}.

Similarly, the proof of the estimates in Lemma
4.5
and in Lemma
4.6
(see also Remark
4.7
) actually show that

\|\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C2},\texttt{D},\texttt{E}}_{1}[\omega,\zeta]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C1}}_{1,2}[\omega,\zeta]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|w\|_{B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}\|z\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

and

\sup_{N}\|\psi_{N}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C1}}_{1,1}[\omega,\zeta]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\sup_{N}\|\psi_{N}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C1}}_{1,3}[\omega,\zeta]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|w\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|z\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}},

from which we conclude

\sup_{N}\|\psi_{N}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{C},\texttt{D},\texttt{E}}_{0}[\omega]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\|w\|_{B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}.

It remains to treat
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B}}_{0}[\omega]
; note that we cannot estimate this portion in
\hat{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}
due to logarithmic divergence in
K
(see the footnote to the proof of Lemma
4.9
).
We use Lemma
2.11
with
p=\infty
and
q=2
, then for
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B1}}_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\big\|S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big[\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B1}}_{0}[\omega]\big]\big\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{smallmatrix}N_{1}\sim N_{3}\gg N\sim N_{2}\\
K\lesssim N_{1}\end{smallmatrix}}N\Big\|P_{N}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big[\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
K\leq|\xi_{13}|<2K\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{13}\xi_{23}}\hat{w}_{N_{1}}(\xi_{1})\hat{w}_{N_{2}}(\xi_{2})\hat{\bar{w}}_{N_{3}}(\xi_{3})\Big]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}\sim N_{3}}N_{1}^{1-}\|w_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{N_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{K,N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}N_{2}^{0+}\|w_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\|w\|_{B^{0+}_{\infty,1}},

and for
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B2}}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big\|S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big[\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{B2}}_{0}[\omega]\big]\big\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{smallmatrix}N_{1}\sim N\gg N_{2}\sim N_{3}\\
K\lesssim N_{2}\end{smallmatrix}}N\Big\|P_{N}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big[\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
K\leq|\xi_{23}|<2K\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{\xi_{3}}{\xi_{13}\xi_{23}}\hat{w}_{N_{1}}(\xi_{1})\hat{w}_{N_{2}}(\xi_{2})\hat{\bar{w}}_{N_{3}}(\xi_{3})\Big]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}\sim N}N_{1}\|w_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{K\lesssim N_{2}\sim N_{3}\ll N_{1}}\frac{N_{3}}{N_{1}}\|w_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{N_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

So far, we have proved

\sup_{N}N\big\|P_{N}S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega(t)]\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|w(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big(1+M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big)\|w(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}

(4.20)

for each
t
, and then (
4.18
) follows from the Hölder inequality in
t
.

We next prove (
4.19
).
Recall that

\displaystyle S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]

\displaystyle=\sum_{N\sim N_{1}\sim N_{2}\sim N_{3}}P_{N}\Big\{w_{N_{1}}w_{N_{2}}\partial_{x}\bar{w}_{N_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big[\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|<1\end{smallmatrix}}i\xi_{3}\hat{w}_{N_{1}}(\xi_{1})\hat{w}_{N_{2}}(\xi_{2})\hat{\bar{w}}_{N_{3}}(\xi_{3})\Big]\Big\}.

The
H^{\frac{1}{2}}
norm of the second part is easily estimated by
\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}
; see the argument in the proof of Lemma
4.11
.
For the first part, it suffices to consider

\sum_{N\sim N_{1}\sim N_{2}\sim N_{3}}\Big\|P_{N}\Big[w_{N_{1}}w_{N_{2}}\partial_{x}\bar{w}_{N_{3}}-P_{N_{1}}(w-W_{0})P_{N_{2}}(w-W_{0})P_{N_{3}}\partial_{x}(\overline{w-W_{0}})\Big]\Big\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

where
W_{0}:=S(t)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]
.
By Bernstein and Hölder, the above is estimated by

\displaystyle\sum_{N^{\prime}_{1}\sim N^{\prime}_{2}\sim N^{\prime}_{3}}(N_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\big(\|w_{N^{\prime}_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|P_{N_{1}^{\prime}}W_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\big(\|w_{N^{\prime}_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|P_{N_{2}^{\prime}}W_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|P_{N^{\prime}_{3}}W_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\big(\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|W_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)^{2}\sup_{N}N\|P_{N}W_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Hence, we obtain

\Big\|\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]-\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]]\Big\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\big(\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|W_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)^{2}\Big(\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|W_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\sup_{N}N\|P_{N}W_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big).

From Lemma
4.3
and the estimate (
4.5
) in Lemmas
4.8
,
4.10
, we have

\|W_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\|\mathcal{N}^{\neg\texttt{A}}_{0}[\omega]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{10}\|\mathcal{L}^{j}_{0}[\omega]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}+M^{-1+}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{5}.

Using this estimate and (
4.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]-\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]]\Big\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}\Big(1+M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big)^{5}\Big(1+\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}\Big).

The claim (
4.19
) follows from Hölder in
t
.
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
T\in(0,1]
.
For any
\omega
with
w=S(t)\check{\omega}\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
, we have
\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]\in L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and

\|\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]\|_{L^{1}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{1}\Big(M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w\|_{S_{T}}^{2},\,T^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\|w\|_{S_{T}}^{2})\|w\|_{S_{T}}^{2},\,TM\Big)\|w\|_{S_{T}},

where
\mathcal{P}_{1}(x,y,z):=(1+x^{5})y+xz
.

Proof.

We see
\mathcal{R}[\omega]\in L^{4}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
from Lemma
4.1
;
\mathcal{N}_{R}[\omega]\in L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
from Lemma
4.2
;
\mathcal{N}_{1}[\omega,\mathcal{R}[\omega]]\in L^{4}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
from Lemmas
4.3
and
4.1
;
\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}[\omega]\in L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
from Lemmas
4.5
,
4.6
,
4.9
and Proposition
3.1
;
\mathcal{L}_{R}^{j}[\omega],\mathcal{L}_{1}^{j}[\omega,\mathcal{R}[\omega]]\in L^{4}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{j}[\omega,\mathcal{N}[\omega]]\in L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
from Lemmas
4.8
,
4.10
,
4.1
and Proposition
3.1
;
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega]-\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]]\in L^{4}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
from Lemma
4.13
.
The claimed estimate also follows from the estimates given in these lemmas.
∎

We conclude the first stage of our NFR argument with the following:

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
w\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
be a (distributional) solution of (
3.1
), and let
\omega(t):=\mathcal{F}S(-t)w(t)
.
Then,
\varpi:=\omega-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega]
belongs to
L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
and satisfies the integral equation (
4.16
) in the sense of
C_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
Moreover,
\varpi
belongs to
W^{1,2}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and satisfies the differential equation

\partial_{t}\varpi=\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]+\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]

(4.21)

in the sense of
L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Proof.

As we have seen in Subsection
4.5
,
\omega
belongs to
L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
, and thus
\varpi
belongs to the same space by Lemma
4.13
.
It is not hard to show the estimate

\|\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}

(this is the special case
J=1
of Lemma
5.5
below), which implies
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]\in L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for any
\varpi\in L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and thus
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]\in W^{1,\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\subset C_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
From Lemma
4.14
, we have
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]\in W^{1,2}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\subset C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
Hence, the equation (
4.16
) holds pointwise in
t\in[-T,T]
as
\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
-valued functions, while the equation (
4.21
) holds in
L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
∎

5
Normal form reduction: II. Infinite reductions

In the second stage, we implement infinite NFR iteration scheme on the equation (
4.16
) for
\varpi
to derive a limit equation which does not have derivative losses.
Using it, we shall prove Theorem
1.1
in Subsection
5.4
.

5.1
Description of general steps via the tree notation

We recall some notation for the infinite NFR argument from
[
8
]
.

Definition 5.1

(Trees)
.

For
J\in\mathbb{N}
, a tree
\mathcal{T}
of the
J
-th generation means a partially ordered set (with a partial order
\succeq
) of
3J+1
elements satisfying all the following conditions.

\mathrm{(i)}

There is the unique element
r\in\mathcal{T}
called the “root” such that
r\succeq a
for all
a\in\mathcal{T}
.

\mathrm{(ii)}

We say
a\in\mathcal{T}
is a “parent” of
b\in\mathcal{T}
and
b
is a “child” of
a
if
a\neq b
,
a\succeq b
, and also
a\succeq c\succeq b
implies
c=a
or
c=b
.
(We write
\mathcal{T}^{0}
to denote the subset of
\mathcal{T}
consisting of all elements that have a child, and
\mathcal{T}^{\infty}:=\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{0}
.)
Then, each element of
\mathcal{T}\setminus\{\text{root}\}
has exactly one parent, whereas each element of
\mathcal{T}^{0}
has exactly three children.
(This condition implies that
\#\mathcal{T}^{0}=J
and
\#\mathcal{T}^{\infty}=2J+1
.)

\mathrm{(iii)}

The elements of
\mathcal{T}^{0}
are numbered from
1
to
J
so that
a^{j_{1}}\succeq a^{j_{2}}
implies
j_{1}\leq j_{2}
, where we write the
j
-th parent (the
j
-th element of
\mathcal{T}^{0}
) as
a^{j}
.
We also distinguish the three children of
a^{j}
and write them as
a^{j}_{1},a^{j}_{2},a^{j}_{3}
.

We write
\mathfrak{T}(J)
to denote the set of all trees of the
J
-th generation.

We note that the above set of trees
\mathfrak{T}(J)
also has an inductive definition:

•

Let
\mathfrak{T}(1)
be the set of one tree consisting of the root and its three children.

•

With
\mathfrak{T}(J-1)
given, define
\mathfrak{T}(J)
as the set of all trees that can be obtained from some
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J-1)
by choosing an element
a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}
as the
J
-th parent and adding its three children.

From this definition, we easily see that
\#\mathfrak{T}(J)=\prod_{j=1}^{J}(2j-1)
.

Definition 5.2

(Index functions)
.

Given
J\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)
, an index function
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{a})_{a\in\mathcal{T}}
on
\mathcal{T}
is a map from
\mathcal{T}
to
\hat{\mathcal{M}}
such that

\xi_{a^{j}}=\xi_{a^{j}_{1}}+\xi_{a^{j}_{2}}+\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\qquad(j=1,\dots,J).

We write
\Xi(\mathcal{T})
to denote the set of all index functions on
\mathcal{T}
, and for
\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}
, denote by
\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})
all index functions with the value
\xi
at the root.
We divide
\mathcal{T}
into two disjoint subsets
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
by the following rule:

a^{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{1},\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} a^{j}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}\quad&\Rightarrow\quad a^{j}_{1},a^{j}_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}~~\text{and}~~a^{j}_{3}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}\\
a^{j}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}\quad&\Rightarrow\quad a^{j}_{1},a^{j}_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}~~\text{and}~~a^{j}_{3}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}\end{aligned}\right.\qquad(j=1,\dots,J).

Write
\mathcal{T}^{0}_{m}:=\mathcal{T}^{0}\cap\mathcal{T}_{m}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{m}:=\mathcal{T}^{\infty}\cap\mathcal{T}_{m}
for
m=1,2
.
Given
\bm{\xi}\in\Xi(\mathcal{T})
and for
j=1,\dots,J
, we define

\phi_{j}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\xi_{a^{j}}^{2}-\xi_{a^{j}_{1}}^{2}-\xi_{a^{j}_{2}}^{2}+\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}^{2}&&\text{if $a^{j}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{0}$,}\\
-\big(&\xi_{a^{j}}^{2}-\xi_{a^{j}_{1}}^{2}-\xi_{a^{j}_{2}}^{2}+\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}^{2}\big)&\quad&\text{if $a^{j}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}^{0}$,}\end{aligned}\right.\quad\qquad\tilde{\phi}_{j}:=\sum_{k=1}^{j}\phi_{k},

and

\Psi_{j}:=\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{a^{j}_{1}a^{j}_{3}}|\wedge|\xi_{a^{j}_{2}a^{j}_{3}}|\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{smallmatrix}N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\geq 1\\
N\sim N_{1}\sim N_{2}\sim N_{3}\end{smallmatrix}}\psi_{N}(\xi_{a^{j}})\psi_{N_{1}}(\xi_{a^{j}_{1}})\psi_{N_{2}}(\xi_{a^{j}_{2}})\psi_{N_{3}}(\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}).

By the above definition, an index function
\bm{\xi}\in\Xi(\mathcal{T})
is determined uniquely by its values on
\mathcal{T}^{\infty}
.
Moreover, we see that
\#\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}=J+1
,
\#\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}=J
, and

\xi=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\xi_{a},\qquad\tilde{\phi}_{J}=\xi^{2}-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}}\xi_{a}^{2}+\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}}\xi_{a^{\prime}}^{2}

for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)
,
\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})
.
We also note that

|\Psi_{j}|\leq 1,\qquad{\rm supp}\,\Psi_{j}\subset\{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi(\mathcal{T}):\langle\xi_{a^{j}}\rangle\sim\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{1}}\rangle\sim\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{2}}\rangle\sim\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\rangle\}.

Using the tree notation, we can explicitly write down the multilinear terms arising during the NFR argument (up to a harmless multiplicative constant), as we describe below.
In the following we
formally
perform the computations, and the justification will be addressed in Subsection
5.3
.

The initial cubic term

\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi](\xi)=\sum_{N\sim N_{1}\sim N_{2}\sim N_{3}}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|\geq 1\end{smallmatrix}}e^{it(\xi^{2}-\xi_{1}^{2}-\xi_{2}^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2})}\psi_{N}(\xi)\xi_{3}\varpi_{N_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varpi_{N_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varpi_{N_{3}}^{*}(\xi_{3})

in the equation (
4.16
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi],

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi](\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\Psi_{1}\xi_{a^{1}_{3}}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}}\varpi(\xi_{a})\prod_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}}\varpi^{*}(\xi_{a^{\prime}}).

By NFR, as we did for the other type of cubic terms, we decompose it as
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}=\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{R}+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{N\!R}
and apply an integration by parts as
\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{N\!R}[\varpi]=\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{0}[\varpi]+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{1}[\varpi,\partial_{t}\varpi]
.
These terms can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{R}[\varpi]=\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi],\qquad\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{0}[\varpi]=\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi],

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}_{1}[\varpi,\zeta]=\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\zeta],

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi](\xi)&:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\Psi_{1}\xi_{a^{1}_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|\leq M}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}}\varpi(\xi_{a})\prod_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}}\varpi^{*}(\xi_{a^{\prime}}),\\
\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi](\xi)&:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\Psi_{1}\xi_{a^{1}_{3}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|>M}}{\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}}\varpi(\xi_{a})\prod_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}}\varpi^{*}(\xi_{a^{\prime}}),\\
\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\zeta](\xi)&:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\Psi_{1}\xi_{a^{1}_{3}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|>M}}{\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\\
&\qquad\times\left\{\begin{aligned} \zeta(\xi_{a^{*}})\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}\setminus\{a^{*}\}}\varpi(\xi_{a})\prod_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}}\varpi^{*}(\xi_{a^{\prime}})&\qquad&(\text{if $a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}$}),\\
\zeta^{*}(\xi_{a^{*}})\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{1}}\varpi(\xi_{a})\prod_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}\setminus\{a^{*}\}}\varpi^{*}(\xi_{a^{\prime}})&\qquad&(\text{if $a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}_{2}$}).\end{aligned}\right.\end{aligned}

Inserting
\partial_{t}\varpi=\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]
, the quintic term
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi,\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]]
appears.
Recall that
\mathfrak{T}(2)=\{\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*}):\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1),a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}\}
, where
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*})
denotes the tree obtained by assigning
a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}
to the second parent and adding its three children.
Then, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi,\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]](\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{(1;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}\bigg[\varpi,\sum_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T}^{\prime})}[\varpi]\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\in\mathfrak{T}(2)}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\tilde{\mathcal{T}})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{2}}\Psi_{1}\xi_{a^{1}_{3}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|>M}}{\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\Psi_{2}\xi_{a^{2}_{3}}\prod_{a\in\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\infty}_{1}}\varpi(\xi_{a})\prod_{a^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\infty}_{2}}\varpi^{*}(\xi_{a^{\prime}}).

We write the right-hand side as
\sum_{\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\in\mathfrak{T}(2)}\mathcal{N}^{(2;\tilde{\mathcal{T}})}[\varpi]
and obtain the equation of the second generation:

\displaystyle\varpi(t)

\displaystyle=\varpi(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(1)}\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]+\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\Big|_{0}^{t}+\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(1;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]]\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\in\mathfrak{T}(2)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(2;\tilde{\mathcal{T}})}[\varpi].

For notational simplicity, from now on we do not distinguish
\varpi
and
\varpi^{*}
; in fact, the difference will play no role in the following argument.

In a general step (i.e., in the equation of the
J
-th generation obtained after
(J-1)
-times applications of NFR), we have the last term

\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi],

where for
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi](\xi):=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\varpi(\xi_{a}).

The
\bm{\xi}
-integral in the above expression is an integral (or sum) on a
2J
-dimensional hyperplane in
\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{3J+1}
.
We choose a different threshold
M_{j}>1
for the “resonant” part at each step; in fact, we will take
M_{j}:=10^{j-1}M
.
We then carry out the
J
-th NFR as

\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi]=\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]+\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]+\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\partial_{t}\varpi]\qquad(\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi](\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\textbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{J}|\leq M_{J}}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\varpi(\xi_{a}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi](\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\varpi(\xi_{a}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\zeta](\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{J}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\zeta(\xi_{a^{*}})\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}\setminus\{a^{*}\}}\varpi(\xi_{a}).

Inserting the equation
\partial_{t}\varpi=\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]+\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi]
, we have

\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\partial_{t}\varpi]=\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]]+\mathcal{N}^{(J+1;\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*}))}[\varpi],

where
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*})\in\mathfrak{T}(J+1)
is the tree obtained by assigning
a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}
to the
(J+1)
-th parent and adding its three children.
Since
\mathfrak{T}(J+1)=\{\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*}):\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J),\,a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}\}
, the equation of the
(J+1)
-th generation is given by

\displaystyle\varpi(t)

\displaystyle=\varpi(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]

(5.1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(j)}\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(j;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]+\mathcal{N}^{(j;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\Big|_{0}^{t}+\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(j;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]]\Big\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\in\mathfrak{T}(J+1)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(J+1;\tilde{\mathcal{T}})}[\varpi].

We will see that the terms in the second line of (
5.1
) are estimated in
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, while the last term
\mathcal{N}^{(J+1;\tilde{\mathcal{T}})}[\varpi]
is still out of the
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
control.
However, as
J\to\infty
we
formally
obtain the limit equation

\displaystyle\varpi(t)

\displaystyle=\varpi(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]

(5.2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(j)}\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(j;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]+\mathcal{N}^{(j;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\Big|_{0}^{t}+\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(j;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]]\Big\},

in which the last term disappears.
In particular, the limit equation (
5.2
) has no derivative losses.

5.2
Multilinear estimates

We give multilinear estimates of the terms appearing in (
5.1
).

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists
C>0
independent of
J,\mathcal{T},M
such that for any
T\in(0,1]
,
J\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)
, we have

\displaystyle\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}\Big)\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M(CM^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2})^{J}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}\Big)\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq(CM^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2})^{J}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Remark 5.2
.

The factor
\prod\limits_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}
will be used to sum up in
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)
; in fact, we see
\#\mathfrak{T}(J)=\prod\limits_{j=1}^{J}(2j-1)\leq\prod\limits_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}
.
Note that
\#\mathfrak{T}(J)
is of
O(C^{J\log J})
but not of
O(C^{J})
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

The proof is a generalization of the argument for
J=1
given in Lemmas
4.2
,
4.3
.
Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the
\bm{\xi}
-integral, we bound
\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}
and
\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}
by

\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2J+1}\times\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle}{\prod\limits_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\langle\xi_{a}\rangle}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}^{2}|\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}|^{2}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{J}|\leq M_{J}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
&\sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle}{\prod\limits_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\langle\xi_{a}\rangle}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\Psi_{j}^{2}|\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{aligned}\right.

respectively.
To estimate the inside of
\sup_{\xi}
, we first notice that

\frac{\langle\xi_{a^{j}}\rangle|\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}|^{2}}{\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{1}}\rangle\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{2}}\rangle\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\rangle}\Psi_{j}\leq C,\qquad j=1,\dots,J,

which shows

\frac{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle}{\prod\limits_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\langle\xi_{a}\rangle}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}|\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}|^{2}=\prod_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\langle\xi_{a^{j}}\rangle|\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}|^{2}}{\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{1}}\rangle\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{2}}\rangle\langle\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\rangle}\Psi_{j}\leq C^{J}.

(5.3)

Next, we prepare the following lemma:

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{T}
.
For any
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|\geq 1\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{\langle\phi_{*}+2\xi_{13}\xi_{23}\rangle^{1+\varepsilon}}\leq C\langle\phi_{*}\rangle^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}

for any
\xi,\phi_{*}\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}
.

Proof.

We may assume
|\phi_{*}|\gg 1
and restrict the domain of the integral to
\{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}):|\phi_{*}+2(\xi-\xi_{1})(\xi-\xi_{2})|\ll|(\xi-\xi_{1})(\xi-\xi_{2})|\sim|\phi_{*}|\}
.
In the non-periodic case, we can estimate the integral as

\iint_{\begin{smallmatrix}|\eta_{1}|,|\eta_{2}|\geq 1\\
|\phi_{*}+2\eta_{1}\eta_{2}|\ll|\eta_{1}\eta_{2}|\sim|\phi_{*}|\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{d\eta_{1}\,d\eta_{2}}{\langle\phi_{*}+2\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\rangle^{1+}}\leq\int_{1\leq|\eta_{1}|\lesssim|\phi_{*}|}\frac{1}{2|\eta_{1}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{d\eta_{2}^{\prime}}{\langle\eta_{2}^{\prime}\rangle^{1+}}\,d\eta_{1}\lesssim|\phi_{*}|^{0+},

as claimed.
In the periodic case, the integral (sum) is bounded by

\sum_{\nu\in[-\frac{|\phi_{*}|}{2},\frac{|\phi_{*}|}{2}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\#\{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}):2(\xi-\xi_{1})(\xi-\xi_{2})=-\phi_{*}+\nu\}}{\langle\nu\rangle^{1+}},

and the estimate follows from the divisor bound

\#\{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}):2(\xi-\xi_{1})(\xi-\xi_{2})=-\phi_{*}+\nu\}\lesssim|{-}\phi_{*}+\nu|^{0+}\sim|\phi_{*}|^{0+}.\qed

By Lemma
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|\geq 1\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\phi_{*}+2\xi_{13}\xi_{23}|>M}}{|\phi_{*}+2\xi_{13}\xi_{23}|^{1+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha}M^{-\alpha+}\langle\phi_{*}\rangle^{0+}\qquad(\alpha>0)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\xi=\xi_{123}\\
|\xi_{13}|\wedge|\xi_{23}|\geq 1\end{smallmatrix}}\mathbf{1}_{|\phi_{*}+2\xi_{13}\xi_{23}|\leq M}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{1+}\langle\phi_{*}\rangle^{0+},

where the implicit constants can be chosen uniformly in
\xi,\phi_{*}\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}
and
M>1
.
Using these estimates (with
\alpha={1-}
or
\alpha=1
) repeatedly, we obtain that

\left\{\begin{aligned} \sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{J}|\leq M_{J}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}&\leq C^{J}\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}M_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\cdot M_{J}^{\frac{1}{2}+}=C^{J}M_{J}\prod_{j=1}^{J}M_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}+},\\
\sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}&\leq C^{J}\prod_{j=1}^{J}M_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}+},\end{aligned}\right.

where we write
\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}
to indicate the restriction to
\{|\xi_{a^{j}_{1}a^{j}_{3}}|\wedge|\xi_{a^{j}_{2}a^{j}_{3}}|\geq 1\}
.
So far, we have obtained the bounds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{J}M_{J}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}M_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\Big)\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2J+1},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{J}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}M_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\Big)\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2J+1}.

Now, we insert
M_{j}=10^{j-1}M
and use
10^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\leq 3^{-1}
to get the bounds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq M\big(10CM^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\big)^{J}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}\Big)^{-1}\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(CM^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\big)^{J}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}\Big)^{-1}\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

The proof of the estimate of
\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{0}[\varpi]
in Lemma
5.1
immediately implies the following:

Lemma 5.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}\Big)\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}_{1}[\varpi,\zeta]\|_{L^{1}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq(CM^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2})^{J}\|\zeta\|_{L^{1}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

We conclude this subsection with the following weak (
\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
) estimate of
\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi]
:

Lemma 5.5
.

We have

\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J}3^{j-1}\Big)\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq C(CM^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2})^{J-1}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{3}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the estimate of
\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]
in Lemma
5.1
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz and (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi]\|_{\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2J+1}\sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{1}{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle\prod\limits_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\langle\xi_{a}\rangle}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}^{2}|\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}|^{2}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{J}\|\varpi\|_{\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2J+1}\sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{1}{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle^{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now, we have
\langle\xi_{a^{J}_{l}}\rangle\leq C^{J}\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle
for
l=1,2
in the support of
\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}
, and hence

\frac{1}{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle^{2}}\int_{\xi_{a^{J}}=\xi_{a^{J}_{1}a^{J}_{2}a^{J}_{3}}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\leq 4C^{2J}.

Using this inequality instead of

\int_{\xi_{a^{J}}=\xi_{a^{J}_{1}a^{J}_{2}a^{J}_{3}}}\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{J-1}+\phi_{J}|\leq M_{J}}\lesssim M_{J}^{1+}\langle\tilde{\phi}_{J-1}\rangle^{0+}

for the previous estimate of
\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}_{R}[\varpi]
, we see that

\sup_{\xi\in\hat{\mathcal{M}}}\Big(\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{1}{\langle\xi_{a^{1}}\rangle^{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}^{N\!R}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C^{J}\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}M_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\leq C^{J}\big(M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\big)^{J-1}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}3^{j-1}\Big)^{-1}.

This implies the desired estimate.
∎

5.3
Justification of formal computations

Here, we give a justification to the derivation of each generation (
5.1
) and the limit equation (
5.2
).
The basic idea is the same as in Subsection
4.5
.
According to Corollary
4.15
, let
\varpi\in L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap C_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}\cap W^{1,2}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
satisfy the equation (of the first generation) (
4.16
) and its differential form (
4.21
).
We regard
\mathcal{W}_{1}[\omega]
as a given forcing term in
L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Assuming that the
J
-th generation equation is given, let us see how to justify the transformation of the last term
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi(t^{\prime})]
for each fixed
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}(J)
.
Since all the frequencies are comparable in this term, it is quite easy to see that the integral in
(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})
is absolutely convergent for any
(t,\xi)\in[-T,T]\times\hat{\mathcal{M}}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\bigg|\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\cdot\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\varpi(t^{\prime},\xi_{a})\bigg|\,dt^{\prime}

(5.4)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle\xi\rangle^{J+}\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\langle\xi_{a}\rangle^{0-}|\varpi(t^{\prime},\xi_{a})|\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle\xi\rangle^{J+}\int_{-T}^{T}\big\|\langle\xi\rangle^{0-}\varpi(t^{\prime})\big\|_{L^{1}}^{2J-1}\big\|\langle\xi\rangle^{0-}\varpi(t^{\prime})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle\xi\rangle^{J+}T\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2J+1}<\infty.

We divide the
\bm{\xi}
-integral, and for the non-resonant part

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it^{\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{J}}f(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})\,dt^{\prime},\qquad f(t,\bm{\xi}):=\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{J}|>M_{J}}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\varpi(t,\xi_{a}),

we justify the integration by parts operation in the following lemma:

Lemma 5.6
.

We have

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}e^{it^{\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{J}}f(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}=\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{J}}}{i\tilde{\phi}_{J}}f(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})\bigg|_{0}^{t}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{e^{it^{\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{J}}}{i\tilde{\phi}_{J}}g(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})\,dt^{\prime}

for any
(t,\xi)\in[-T,T]\times\hat{\mathcal{M}}
, where

g(t,\bm{\xi}):=\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Psi_{j}\xi_{a^{j}_{3}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{J-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{j}|>M_{j}}}{\tilde{\phi}_{j}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{\phi}_{J}|>M_{J}}\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}(\partial_{t}\varpi)(t,\xi_{a^{*}})\prod_{a\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}\setminus\{a^{*}\}}\varpi(t,\xi_{a}).

Proof.

The proof is parallel to that of Lemma
4.12
, so we omit the detailed estimates.
We shall prove the convergences corresponding to (
4.12
)–(
4.14
).
For (
4.12
), it suffices to show

\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}|f(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})|\,dt^{\prime}<\infty,

which we have already seen in (
5.4
).
For (
4.13
), it suffices to show the continuity of the map

[-T,T]\ni\ t\quad\mapsto\quad\langle\xi\rangle^{-J-}\int_{\bm{\xi}\in\Xi_{\xi}(\mathcal{T})}\frac{e^{it\tilde{\phi}_{J}}}{i\tilde{\phi}_{J}}f(t,\bm{\xi})\ \in L^{\infty}_{\xi}.

This is also verified by estimating as (
5.4
); we slightly modify it to have the
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
norm for
\varpi(t)
’s, so that we can use the continuity of
\varpi(t)
in
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
.
The convergence corresponding to (
4.14
) can be shown in a similar manner; this time we multiply the integral by
\langle\xi\rangle^{-J-1-}
and argue as (
5.4
) to measure
\frac{1}{h}(\varpi(t^{\prime}+h)-\varpi(t^{\prime}))-(\partial_{t}\varpi)(t^{\prime})
and
(\partial_{t}\varpi)(t^{\prime})
in
L^{2}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
while
\varpi(t^{\prime}+h)-\varpi(t^{\prime})
in
L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}-}
.
∎

Inserting (
4.21
), we have so far justified the equality

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi(t^{\prime})]\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{R}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi(t^{\prime})]\,dt^{\prime}+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{(J;\mathcal{T})}[\varpi(t^{\prime})]\bigg|_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{a^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\infty}}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}[\varpi(t^{\prime}),\mathcal{W}_{1}(t^{\prime})]\,dt^{\prime}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{(J;\mathcal{T},a^{*})}[\varpi(t^{\prime}),\mathcal{N}^{\texttt{A}}[\varpi(t^{\prime})]]\,dt^{\prime}\Big\}\end{aligned}

pointwise for
(t,\xi)\in[-T,T]\times\hat{\mathcal{M}}
.
Each term on the right-hand side is well-defined for any
(t,\xi)
as an absolutely convergent integral in
(t^{\prime},\bm{\xi})
or in
\bm{\xi}
.
The last term can be rewritten as
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}^{(J+1;\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*}))}[\varpi(t^{\prime})]\,dt^{\prime}
, which is an absolutely convergent integral over the index functions on the extended tree
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*})\in\mathfrak{T}(J+1)
, by the estimate (
5.4
) for
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}(a^{*})
and Fubini’s theorem.
Repeating this procedure, we justify the equation (
5.1
) for any generation
J\geq 1
.

Finally, we assume that
M>1
is large and
T\in(0,1]
is small so that

M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\ll 1,\qquad TM\leq 1.

(5.5)

Then, by Lemmas
5.1
,
5.4
,
5.5
, as
J\to\infty
the second line of (
5.1
) converges in
L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and the third line vanishes in
L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
This verifies that the limit equation (
5.2
) holds pointwise in
t\in[-T,T]
as
\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
-valued functions.

5.4
Proof of the main theorem

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
1.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

As we mentioned before, it suffices to prove uniqueness of the (distributional) solution
w
to (
3.1
) in the class
L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
.
Moreover, it is enough to show the uniqueness on the interval
[-T^{\prime},T^{\prime}]
for some
T^{\prime}\in(0,T]
(we write
T^{\prime}
as
T
below).
Let
w_{1},w_{2}
be two such solutions with
w_{1}(0)=w_{2}(0)
, and define
\omega_{m}:=\mathcal{F}S(-t)w_{m}
and
\varpi_{m}:=\omega_{m}-\mathcal{W}_{0}[\omega_{m}]
,
m=1,2
.
Then, each of
\varpi_{m}
is a solution of (
4.16
) as stated in Corollary
4.15
.
Choose
M\gg 1
and
0<T\ll 1
according to
\|w_{m}\|_{S_{T}}
,
m=1,2
so that

TM\leq 1,\qquad\rho_{0}:=C\sum_{m=1,2}\big(M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|w_{m}\|_{S_{T}}^{2}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\|w_{m}\|_{S_{T}}^{2})\|w_{m}\|_{S_{T}}^{2}\big)\ll 1.

(5.6)

Then, from the estimate (
4.17
) in Lemma
4.13
(and its difference version) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|w_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\|\varpi_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 2\|w_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\|\varpi_{1}-\varpi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 2\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

In particular, the assumption (
5.6
) implies (
5.5
) for
\varpi_{m}
,
m=1,2
, and thus
\varpi_{m}
satisfies the limit equation (
5.2
).
We now take the difference of the limit equation for
\varpi_{1}
,
\varpi_{2}
and apply the difference versions of Lemmas
4.14
,
5.1
and
5.4
, to see that

\|\varpi_{1}-\varpi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\rho_{0}\big(\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}+\|\varpi_{1}-\varpi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big),

and hence,

\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 2\|\varpi_{1}-\varpi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\hat{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\rho_{0}\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}.

On the other hand, from Proposition
2.8
we have

\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}}\leq CT^{\frac{1}{4}-}\big(1+\|w_{1}\|_{S_{T}}^{4}+\|w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}^{4}\big)\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}

if
T
is modified appropriately.
From these estimates, we have
\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{S_{T}}
, which establishes the uniqueness for (
3.1
) in
L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap L^{4}_{T}B^{0+}_{\infty,1}
.
∎

Appendix A
Weak solutions

Let us consider the inhomogeneous linear Schrödinger equation

\partial_{t}u(t,x)=i\partial_{x}^{2}u(t,x)+\partial_{x}F(t,x),\qquad(t,x)\in(-T,T)\times\mathcal{M},

(A.1)

where
\mathcal{M}
is either
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{T}
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
s,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
be
\sigma<s
.
Suppose that
u\in L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{s}(\mathcal{M}))\cap L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}((-T,T)\times\mathcal{M})
,
F\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}((-T,T)\times\mathcal{M})
,
\partial_{x}F\in L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M}))
, and
u,F
satisfy (
A.1
) in the sense of distributions; i.e.,

-\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{M}}u(t,x)(\partial_{t}\psi)(t,x)\,dx\,dt=\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big\{iu(t,x)(\partial_{x}^{2}\psi)(t,x)-F(t,x)(\partial_{x}\psi)(t,x)\Big\}\,dx\,dt

(A.2)

for any
\psi\in\mathcal{D}((-T,T)\times\mathcal{M})
.
14
14

14

Although we will use the same notation for
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{T}
, when
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}
we actually consider the extended class of test functions
\mathcal{D}(-T,T;\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}))
, where
\mathcal{S}
is the Schwartz space.
Then, the
L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
assumption on
u,F
should be regarded as
L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(-T,T;L^{1}_{\mathrm{poly}}(\mathbb{R}))
, where
L^{1}_{\mathrm{poly}}
is the set of
L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
functions growing at most polynomially at infinity.

Then, there exists a (unique) function

\tilde{u}\in C([-T,T];H^{s-}(\mathcal{M}))\cap C_{w}([-T,T];H^{s}(\mathcal{M}))\cap W^{1,\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))

(where
\sigma^{\prime}:=\min\{s-2,\sigma\}
) such that

1.

\tilde{u}(t)=u(t)
(in
H^{s}(\mathcal{M})
) for a.e.
t\in(-T,T)
.

2.

\tilde{u}
and
F
satisfy (
A.1
) in
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
for a.e.
t\in(-T,T)
.

3.

\tilde{u}(t)=S(t-t_{0})\tilde{u}(t_{0})+\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}S(t-t^{\prime})\partial_{x}F(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}
holds in
H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
for any
t,t_{0}\in[-T,T]
.

Proof.

First, we show the identity

-\int_{-T}^{T}u(t)\eta^{\prime}(t)\,dt=\int_{-T}^{T}\Big\{i\partial_{x}^{2}u(t)+\partial_{x}F(t)\Big\}\eta(t)\,dt

for any
\eta\in\mathcal{D}(-T,T)
, where the integrals are considered as those of
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
-valued functions.
To see this, let
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{M})
, and observe that

\Big\langle{-}\int_{-T}^{T}u(t)\eta^{\prime}(t)\,dt,\,\phi\Big\rangle=-\int_{-T}^{T}\big\langle u(t)\eta^{\prime}(t),\,\phi\big\rangle\,dt=-\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{M}}u(t,x)(\partial_{t}\psi)(t,x)\,dx\,dt,

where
\psi(t,x):=\eta(t)\phi(x)\in\mathcal{D}((-T,T)\times\mathcal{M})
.
At the first equality, we have used the fact that
u(t)\eta^{\prime}(t)\in L^{1}(-T,T;H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))
and that the evaluation map
u\mapsto\langle u,\phi\rangle
is a bounded linear functional on
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
and hence commutes with the
t
-integral.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\Big\langle\int_{-T}^{T}\big\{i\partial_{x}^{2}u(t)+\partial_{x}F(t)\big\}\eta(t)\,dt,\,\phi\Big\rangle=\int_{-T}^{T}\big\langle i\partial_{x}^{2}u(t)+\partial_{x}F(t),\,\phi\big\rangle\eta(t)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-T}^{T}\Big\{i\big\langle u(t),\phi^{\prime\prime}\big\rangle-\big\langle F(t),\,\phi^{\prime}\big\rangle\Big\}\eta(t)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big\{iu(t,x)(\partial_{x}^{2}\psi)(t,x)-F(t,x)(\partial_{x}\psi)(t,x)\Big\}\,dx\,dt,

and hence, by (
A.2
) we have

\Big\langle{-}\int_{-T}^{T}u(t)\eta^{\prime}(t)\,dt,\,\phi\Big\rangle=\Big\langle\int_{-T}^{T}\big\{i\partial_{x}^{2}u(t)+\partial_{x}F(t)\big\}\eta(t)\,dt,\,\phi\Big\rangle

for any
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{M})
, showing the claim.
In particular,
u
satisfies (
A.1
) as a
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
-valued distribution on
(-T,T)
.
Since the right-hand side of (
A.1
) belongs to
L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))
, we see that
u\in W^{1,\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))
and (
A.1
) holds in
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
for
a.e.

t\in(-T,T)
.
Then, since
v(t):=u(t)-\int_{0}^{t}(\partial_{t}u)(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}
satisfies
\partial_{t}v=0
in
L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))
, there exists
u_{0}\in H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
such that
v(t)=u_{0}
for
a.e.

t\in(-T,T)
.
Setting
\tilde{u}(t):=u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\partial_{t}u)(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}\in C([-T,T];H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))
, we have
u(t)=\tilde{u}(t)
for
a.e.

t\in(-T,T)
.

From now on, we write
\tilde{u}
as
u
.
Recalling
u\in L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{s}(\mathcal{M}))
, let
J\subset(-T,T)
denote the set of full measure in which
\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}}
.
Given any
t\in[-T,T]
, we approximate it by
\{t_{n}\}_{n}\subset J
.
Then, for any
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{M})
,
\{(u(t_{n}),\phi)_{H^{s}}\}_{n}
is a Cauchy sequence, by continuity of
u(t)
in
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
, and thus it converges to
(u(t),\phi)_{H^{s}}
.
This means that
|(u(t),\phi)_{H^{s}}|\leq\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}}\|\phi\|_{H^{s}}
for any
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{M})
, and therefore
u(t)\in H^{s}(\mathcal{M})
,
\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}}
for all
t\in[-T,T]
and
u\in C_{w}([-T,T];H^{s}(\mathcal{M}))
.
By interpolation between
H^{s}
and
H^{\sigma^{\prime}}
, we also show that
u\in C([-T,T];H^{s-}(\mathcal{M}))
.

Finally, set
v(t^{\prime}):=S(-t^{\prime})u(t^{\prime})
for
t^{\prime}\in[-T,T]
.
Using (
A.2
) with
\psi(t^{\prime},x)=\eta(t^{\prime})S(-t^{\prime})\phi(x)
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\langle{-}\int_{-T}^{T}v(t^{\prime})\eta^{\prime}(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime},\,\phi\Big\rangle=-\int_{-T}^{T}\big\langle u(t^{\prime}),\,S(-t^{\prime})\phi\big\rangle\eta^{\prime}(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{M}}u(t^{\prime},x)\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big[\eta(t^{\prime})S(-t^{\prime})\phi\big](x)\,dx\,dt+\int_{-T}^{T}\big\langle u(t^{\prime}),\,-i\partial_{x}^{2}S(-t^{\prime})\phi\big\rangle\eta(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{M}}F(t^{\prime},x)\eta(t^{\prime})\big[\partial_{x}S(-t^{\prime})\phi\big](x)\,dx\,dt=\Big\langle\int_{-T}^{T}S(-t^{\prime})\partial_{x}F(t^{\prime})\eta(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime},\,\phi\Big\rangle

for any
\eta\in\mathcal{D}(-T,T)
and
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{M})
.
Since
S(-\cdot)\partial_{x}F(\cdot)\in L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M}))
, this shows
v\in W^{1,\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M}))
and
\partial_{t^{\prime}}v(t^{\prime})=S(-t^{\prime})\partial_{x}F(t^{\prime})
in
L^{\infty}(-T,T;H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M}))
.
Similarly to the previous argument, for any
t_{0}\in[-T,T]
we see that
w(t):=v(t)-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\partial_{t^{\prime}}v)(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}
coincides with some
v_{0}\in H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
for
a.e.

t
.
Recalling
v(t^{\prime})=S(-t^{\prime})u(t^{\prime})\in C([-T,T];H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M}))
, we see
v_{0}=v(t_{0})
and the equality
w(t)=v(t_{0})
, which can be rewritten as

S(-t)u(t)=S(-t_{0})u(t_{0})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}S(-t^{\prime})\partial_{x}F(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}

in
H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
, holds for all
t,t_{0}\in[-T,T]
.
We obtain the desired integral equation for
u(t)
by applying
S(t)
.
∎
